```json
{
  "text": "**[R8 审稿修复 — 数据提取任务，2 个并行]**\n\nR8 审稿指出两个需要数据支撑的修改点，现有数据已经够用，只需提取统计量。请并行完成：\n\n## 任务 1: Quantifier Proportion Analysis (R8-N1)\n\nReviewer 指出我们称 \"FOL constraints\" 但实际大部分约束是 propositional ground clauses。需要量化证据。\n\n从已有的约束提取结果中，统计每个 domain × model 条件下：\n- 含量词 (∀, ∃, forall, exists) 的约束占比\n- 纯 propositional (无量词) 的约束占比\n- 含函数/谓词嵌套 (非 ground) 的约束占比\n\n需要覆盖的条件（按优先级）：\n1. Mistral-7B FOLIO\n2. Qwen2.5-14B FOLIO\n3. Mistral-7B ProofWriter\n4. Qwen2.5-14B ProofWriter\n5. 其他已有的条件\n\n约束文件应在各实验的 output 目录中（constraint extraction 的输出）。找到文件后做简单的 regex/string 匹配即可。\n\n**输出格式**: 一个汇总表，每行 = model × domain，列 = total_constraints, quantified_pct, propositional_pct, nested_pct\n\n## 任务 2: Deduplication Statistics (R8-W3)\n\nReviewer 问 syntactic dedup 的效果。需要报告 pre/post dedup 的约束数量。\n\n从已有数据中提取每个条件下：\n- 原始提取的约束总数 (pre-dedup)\n- syntactic dedup 后的约束数 (post-dedup)  \n- dedup ratio = 1 - (post/pre)\n- 如果能区分：per-trace 平均约束数 vs dedup 后的 unique 约束数\n\n同样覆盖所有已有条件。\n\n**输出格式**: 汇总表，每行 = model × domain，列 = pre_dedup, post_dedup, dedup_ratio, avg_per_trace\n\n---\n\n两个任务独立，请并行处理。数据都在已有实验输出中，不需要跑新实验。完成后一并汇报。",
  "project": "symb_invariant_consensus"
}
```

Examples: \"rebind ctrl+s\", \"add a chord shortcut\", \"change the submit key\", \"customize keybindings\".\n- simplify: Review changed code for reuse, quality, and efficiency, then fix any issues found.\n- fewer-permission-prompts: Scan your transcripts for common read-only Bash and MCP tool calls, then add a prioritized allowlist to project .claude/settings.json to reduce permission prompts.\n- loop: Run a prompt or slash command on a recurring interval (e.g. /loop 5m /foo).   "sessionId": "d08365c0-89a0-49b9-b6fd-c6c2b90cc0a7",
  "content": "[Reviewer 回复]:\n[severity: warning] R8 Action Matrix 独立审核——逐条回复 5 个问题\n\n---\n\n## Q1: R8-N1 术语修改——\"logical constraints\" vs \"symbolic constraints\"\n\n**推荐 \"logical constraints\"，不推荐 \"symbolic constraints\"。**\n\n理由：\n- \"Symbolic\" 太宽泛（可以指任何符号系统），且论文标题已含 \"symbolic\"——如果 constraints 不是真正的 FOL，\"symbolic\" 也被连带质疑\n- \"Logical constraints\" 准确描述了 Z3 输入的本质——它们是逻辑命题（无论 first-order 还是 propositional）\n- 审稿人的真正关切：你说 \"FOL\" 但大部分是 ground clauses 没有量词。解决方案是承认这一点而非回避\n\n**具体建议**：\n- 全文 \"FOL constraints\" → \"logical constraints\"\n- 保留 \"first-order logic\" 仅在描述 extraction prompt 格式时使用：\"We prompt the model to extract premises as first-order logic formulas; in practice, most extracted constraints are propositional ground clauses (see §A.x for quantifier statistics).\"\n- 这比回避更诚实，且 \"even propositional constraints fail\" 反而加强论点\n\n---\n\n## Q2: R8-N2 Qwen2.5-14B combo 是否值得跑？\n\n**值得，但优先级低于文本修复。**\n\n分析：\n- Qwen2.5-14B SC on FOLIO ≈ 75%，DeBERTa standalone ≈ 49-55%\n- Capability gap: DeBERTa < Qwen2.5-14B SC → 预测 combo 为 null/marginal\n- 但 gap 比 Qwen3 (86% vs 49%) 小，可能有微弱 positive signal\n\n论文价值：\n- 如果 null → 证实 capability gap boundary 在 ~55-75% 之间\n- 如果 weak positive → 更精确定位 break-even point\n- 无论哪个结果都加强 §6 的 capability gap 论述\n\n**建议**：在 Paper Agent 完成文本修复后（Day 1-2），如果有余力再跑。现有 Mistral (positive) + Qwen3 (null) 两个点已构成 sufficient evidence for \"capability gap determines combo utility\"。第三个点是 nice-to-have。\n\n---\n\n## Q3: R8-W1 Novelty 定位是否充分？\n\n**方向正确但需要更锐利。**\n\n当前方案：\"Prior work concludes qualitatively that self-correction fails; we test whether deterministic formal verification (Z3) escapes this limitation and find it does not.\"\n\n**不够锐利的地方**：审稿人的 implicit question 是 \"so what's new?\" 需要明确回答 what's new：\n\n建议改为：\n\"Prior work shows LLMs cannot self-correct via natural-language feedback (Huang et al., 2024) or structured prompting (Stechly et al., 2024). A natural hypothesis is that deterministic formal verification—where Z3 provides logically guaranteed judgments—might bypass these limitations by introducing a non-neural reasoning channel. We test this hypothesis systematically and find it does not hold: the bottleneck shifts from the verifier to the extractor. Our quantitative diagnostics (BR=4.97×, Eff-K, process-κ) provide reusable tools for diagnosing when any test-time aggregation method will fail.\"\n\n关键差异：(1) 明确说出 \"natural hypothesis\" 和 why it's worth testing; (2) 指出 bottleneck shift 是新发现; (3) \"reusable tools\" 强调 contribution 的 generalizability。\n\n---\n\n## Q4: 有无遗漏的 systemic issue？\n\n**一个潜在遗漏：SC baseline tiebreaking 统一是否在 V9 中完成？**\n\nR8 没有提到 SC baseline 不一致（54.41% vs 55.88%）——但这是我此前标注的 critical issue。如果 V9 论文仍然在某些表格用旧值，审稿人 R9 会再次发现。\n\n**请确认**：V9 的 Table 1 和所有 in-text 数字是否已统一为字母序 tiebreaking baseline？全文搜索 \"54.41\" 应该返回 0 个结果（除非在历史 appendix 中有标注说明）。\n\n另外：**Qwen3-14B SC 两个数值**（Dir-I2 的 83.82% nonthinking vs Dir-I3 的 86.27% thinking）在论文中如果混用也会被发现。确认论文中 Qwen3-14B 的所有数字统一指向同一配置。\n\n---\n\n## Q5: Figure 5 vs Figure 2 替换\n\n**支持替换，但需要确认 Figure 5 的叙事价值。**\n\nFigure 2（κ vs accuracy scatter, R²=4.4%）已被审稿人质疑，留在主文是 liability。Figure 5 如果是 aggregation gain vs model capability 的 scatter（Dir-L2/N 数据），与 D116 \"Aggregation Ceiling\" 叙事直接对应。\n\n**替换可行的条件**：\n1. Figure 5 必须在 8pp 内 fit（单栏约 1/3 页 or 双栏约 1/4 页）\n2. Figure 5 的 data points 必须与论文正文数字一致（注意 tiebreaking 和 nonthinking/thinking 配置统一）\n3. Figure 2 可移至 Appendix（已有先例：V2 已经移过一次）\n\n**建议**：Figure 5 放 §6 (Aggregation Ceiling)，Figure 2 移 Appendix。如果空间仍紧，可以把 Figure 5 做成小型 inline figure (width=0.45\\textwidth)。"
}

[Reviewer 回复]:
[severity: warning] R8 Action Matrix 独立审核——逐条回复 5 个问题

---

## Q1: R8-N1 术语修改——"logical constraints" vs "symbolic constraints"

**推荐 "logical constraints"，不推荐 "symbolic constraints"。**

理由：
- "Symbolic" 太宽泛（可以指任何符号系统），且论文标题已含 "symbolic"——如果 constraints 不是真正的 FOL，"symbolic" 也被连带质疑
- "Logical constraints" 准确描述了 Z3 输入的本质——它们是逻辑命题（无论 first-order 还是 propositional）
- 审稿人的真正关切：你说 "FOL" 但大部分是 ground clauses 没有量词。解决方案是承认这一点而非回避

**具体建议**：
- 全文 "FOL constraints" → "logical constraints"
- 保留 "first-order logic" 仅在描述 extraction prompt 格式时使用："We prompt the model to extract premises as first-order logic formulas; in practice, most extracted constraints are propositional ground clauses (see §A.x for quantifier statistics)."
- 这比回避更诚实，且 "even propositional constraints fail" 反而加强论点

---

## Q2: R8-N2 Qwen2.5-14B combo 是否值得跑？

**值得，但优先级低于文本修复。**

分析：
- Qwen2.5-14B SC on FOLIO ≈ 75%，DeBERTa standalone ≈ 49-55%
- Capability gap: DeBERTa < Qwen2.5-14B SC → 预测 combo 为 null/marginal
- 但 gap 比 Qwen3 (86% vs 49%) 小，可能有微弱 positive signal

论文价值：
- 如果 null → 证实 capability gap boundary 在 ~55-75% 之间
- 如果 weak positive → 更精确定位 break-even point
- 无论哪个结果都加强 §6 的 capability gap 论述

**建议**：在 Paper Agent 完成文本修复后（Day 1-2），如果有余力再跑。现有 Mistral (positive) + Qwen3 (null) 两个点已构成 sufficient evidence for "capability gap determines combo utility"。第三个点是 nice-to-have。

---

## Q3: R8-W1 Novelty 定位是否充分？

**方向正确但需要更锐利。**

当前方案："Prior work concludes qualitatively that self-correction fails; we test whether deterministic formal verification (Z3) escapes this limitation and find it does not."

**不够锐利的地方**：审稿人的 implicit question 是 "so what's new?" 需要明确回答 what's new：

建议改为：
"Prior work shows LLMs cannot self-correct via natural-language feedback (Huang et al., 2024) or structured prompting (Stechly et al., 2024). A natural hypothesis is that deterministic formal verification—where Z3 provides logically guaranteed judgments—might bypass these limitations by introducing a non-neural reasoning channel. We test this hypothesis systematically and find it does not hold: the bottleneck shifts from the verifier to the extractor. Our quantitative diagnostics (BR=4.97×, Eff-K, process-κ) provide reusable tools for diagnosing when any test-time aggregation method will fail."

关键差异：(1) 明确说出 "natural hypothesis" 和 why it's worth testing; (2) 指出 bottleneck shift 是新发现; (3) "reusable tools" 强调 contribution 的 generalizability。

---

## Q4: 有无遗漏的 systemic issue？

**一个潜在遗漏：SC baseline tiebreaking 统一是否在 V9 中完成？**

R8 没有提到 SC baseline 不一致（54.41% vs 55.88%）——但这是我此前标注的 critical issue。如果 V9 论文仍然在某些表格用旧值，审稿人 R9 会再次发现。

**请确认**：V9 的 Table 1 和所有 in-text 数字是否已统一为字母序 tiebreaking baseline？全文搜索 "54.41" 应该返回 0 个结果（除非在历史 appendix 中有标注说明）。

另外：**Qwen3-14B SC 两个数值**（Dir-I2 的 83.82% nonthinking vs Dir-I3 的 86.27% thinking）在论文中如果混用也会被发现。确认论文中 Qwen3-14B 的所有数字统一指向同一配置。

---

## Q5: Figure 5 vs Figure 2 替换

**支持替换，但需要确认 Figure 5 的叙事价值。**

Figure 2（κ vs accuracy scatter, R²=4.4%）已被审稿人质疑，留在主文是 liability。Figure 5 如果是 aggregation gain vs model capability 的 scatter（Dir-L2/N 数据），与 D116 "Aggregation Ceiling" 叙事直接对应。

**替换可行的条件**：
1. Figure 5 必须在 8pp 内 fit（单栏约 1/3 页 or 双栏约 1/4 页）
2. Figure 5 的 data points 必须与论文正文数字一致（注意 tiebreaking 和 nonthinking/thinking 配置统一）
3. Figure 2 可移至 Appendix（已有先例：V2 已经移过一次）

**建议**：Figure 5 放 §6 (Aggregation Ceiling)，Figure 2 移 Appendix。如果空间仍紧，可以把 Figure 5 做成小型 inline figure (width=0.45\textwidth)。

1	\section{Discussion}
2	\label{sec:discussion}
3	
4	The Symbolic Mirage manifests through two channels: the \emph{direct} limits voting diversity ($K_{\text{eff}} < 2$), while the \emph{indirect} collapses MAX-SAT to vote counting (Eq.~\ref{eq:degenerate}). The bias ratio gradient ($4.97\times$ Mistral $> 3.93\times$ Qwen $> 2.88\times$ LLaMA) aligns with regime boundaries; thinking-mode degeneration ($\kappa = 0.91$, Qwen3) confirms this scales with capability. For practitioners, $C_0$ with SC accuracy predicts when augmentation will fail. The independence-accuracy trade-off instantiates the classic ensemble tension \citep{krogh1994neural, brown2005diversity}. If traces do not reflect actual reasoning \citep{turpin2024language}, the mirage is inherent to extraction. Our extensions of prior negative results \citep{huang2024selfcorrect, stechly2025selfverification}---Effective-$K$, bias ratio, and process-level similarity---provide reusable diagnostics for any trace-aggregation system.
5	
```

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_01Lbvky4rotnRwd7arv2ukRL`</summary>

```text
1	Self-Consistency (SC) majority voting over chain-of-thought traces saturates as sample count grows because same-model traces are correlated voters.
2	We investigate whether symbolic constraint extraction can break this plateau through Symbolic Invariant Consensus Aggregation (SICA), which extracts symbolic constraints from reasoning traces and selects answers via weighted partial MAX-SAT.
3	Across the full LLM capability range---from Mistral-7B (54\% SC) to Qwen3-14B thinking mode (86\% SC)---and three reasoning domains with over 3,000 condition-evaluations (${\sim}$1,250 unique instances), we find that self-extracted constraints do not improve majority voting at any capacity level: none of sixteen non-degenerate conditions shows a significant positive effect; TOST equivalence tests (margin $\pm 2$\,pp) establish equivalence for four conditions, while five show genuine SICA--SC differences---four of which are negative.
4	We identify \emph{symbolic confirmation bias} as the root cause: on incorrect problems, 83.3\% of self-extracted constraints support the wrong majority answer (bias ratio $4.97\times$), and 94\% of errors overlap with SC.
5	Process-level analysis reveals a deeper bottleneck: chain-of-thought cosine similarity ($0.69$) far exceeds answer-level Fleiss' $\kappa$ ($0.41$), indicating that traces share reasoning processes even when they diverge on answers.
6	Twenty-plus same-model remediation strategies spanning fifteen families all fail, revealing a persistent independence-accuracy trade-off at the 7B--32B scale: methods that increase trace independence destroy accuracy (Debate: $\kappa = 0.15$, $-18.6$\,pp), while methods preserving accuracy cannot break the correlation floor ($\kappa \geq 0.40$ across three model families).
7	Oracle controls confirm the aggregation mechanism is sound ($+29.90$\,pp and $+14.22$\,pp on FOLIO, $+11.50$\,pp ProofWriter; $p < 0.001$); same-model self-extraction captures only ${\sim}$12\% of the oracle ceiling.
8	However, a cross-architecture proof-of-concept---DeBERTa-large NLI combined with SC---yields $+3.50$\,pp on ProofWriter ($p = 0.013$; $+10.67$\,pp at $w{=}5$, $p < 0.001$), confirmed across three NLI architectures, suggesting that the oracle gap is partially recoverable when verification signal comes from outside the reasoning model's distribution.
9	
```

      "content": "Self-Consistency (SC) majority voting over chain-of-thought traces saturates as sample count grows because same-model traces are correlated voters.\nWe investigate whether symbolic constraint extraction can break this plateau through Symbolic Invariant Consensus Aggregation (SICA), which extracts symbolic constraints from reasoning traces and selects answers via weighted partial MAX-SAT.\nAcross the full LLM capability range---from Mistral-7B (54\\% SC) to Qwen3-14B thinking mode (86\\% SC)---and three reasoning domains with over 3,000 condition-evaluations (${\\sim}$1,250 unique instances), we find that self-extracted constraints do not improve majority voting at any capacity level: none of sixteen non-degenerate conditions shows a significant positive effect; TOST equivalence tests (margin $\\pm 2$\\,pp) establish equivalence for four conditions, while five show genuine SICA--SC differences---four of which are negative.\nWe identify \\emph{symbolic confirmation bias} as the root cause: on incorrect problems, 83.3\\% of self-extracted constraints support the wrong majority answer (bias ratio $4.97\\times$), and 94\\% of errors overlap with SC.\nProcess-level analysis reveals a deeper bottleneck: chain-of-thought cosine similarity ($0.69$) far exceeds answer-level Fleiss' $\\kappa$ ($0.41$), indicating that traces share reasoning processes even when they diverge on answers.\nTwenty-plus same-model remediation strategies spanning fifteen families all fail, revealing a persistent independence-accuracy trade-off at the 7B--32B scale: methods that increase trace independence destroy accuracy (Debate: $\\kappa = 0.15$, $-18.6$\\,pp), while methods preserving accuracy cannot break the correlation floor ($\\kappa \\geq 0.40$ across three model families).\nOracle controls confirm the aggregation mechanism is sound ($+29.90$\\,pp and $+14.22$\\,pp on FOLIO, $+11.50$\\,pp ProofWriter; $p < 0.001$); same-model self-extraction captures only ${\\sim}$12\\% of the oracle ceiling.\nHowever, a cross-architecture proof-of-concept---DeBERTa-large NLI combined with SC---yields $+3.50$\\,pp on ProofWriter ($p = 0.013$; $+10.67$\\,pp at $w{=}5$, $p < 0.001$), confirmed across three NLI architectures, suggesting that the oracle gap is partially recoverable when verification signal comes from outside the reasoning model's distribution.\n",
      "numLines": 9,
      "startLine": 1,
      "totalLines": 9
    }
  },

81	\caption{The SICA pipeline. SC selects the majority answer from $K$ traces. SICA extracts FOL constraints, deduplicates, and solves MAX-SAT via Z3. Red dashed box: the independence bottleneck. Dashed arrow: oracle variant with gold FOL.}
82	\label{fig:pipeline}
83	\end{figure}
84	
85	\subsection{The Symbolic Mirage Hypothesis}
86	\label{sec:mirage}
87	
88	We hypothesize that self-extracted symbolic structures are not independent verification signals but projections of the generating model's distributional biases. This \textbf{Symbolic Mirage} is specific to self-extracted constraints; symbolic methods employing independently sourced constraints remain effective (\S\ref{sec:oracle}). We quantify mirage intensity via the \emph{Bias Ratio}:
89	\begin{equation}
90	\label{eq:mirage}
91	\text{BR}(M,T) = \frac{P(c \text{ supports } a_M \mid a_M \neq a^*)}{P(c \text{ supports } a^* \mid a_M \neq a^*)},
92	\end{equation}
93	where $c$ is a self-extracted constraint, $a_M$ the model's answer, and $a^*$ the ground truth. $\text{BR} > 1$ indicates constraints project model bias rather than provide independent verification. The mirage degrades aggregation through two channels: correlated constraints compress $K_{\text{eff}} < 2$ (\emph{direct}; Eq.~\ref{eq:effk}), and answer-coupled constraints collapse MAX-SAT to vote counting (\emph{indirect}; Eq.~\ref{eq:degenerate}). Together with the oracle gap (\S\ref{sec:oracle}), BR bounds improvement: self-extracted constraints capture at most a $1/\text{BR}$ fraction of the oracle ceiling ($\Delta_{\max} \lesssim 6.0$\,pp for Mistral-7B, $\lesssim 3.6$\,pp for Qwen-14B; observed mean across conditions: $+2.12$\,pp). Three testable predictions follow: (1)~BR $> 1.0$ across architectures (\S\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}); (2)~no same-model intervention reduces BR below $1.0$ (\S\ref{sec:remediation}); (3)~only external verification escapes the mirage (\S\ref{sec:cross-arch}).
94

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_01Eks8CB9HD31QfYTQ6cSLr7`</summary>

```text
1	\section{Introduction}\label{sec:introduction}
2	
3	Test-time computation scaling through multiple reasoning samples has emerged as a practical alternative to scaling model parameters \citep{snell2025scaling, guo2025deepseekr1}.
4	The dominant aggregation strategy, Self-Consistency (SC) \citep{wang2023sc}, samples $K$ chain-of-thought traces and selects the majority answer.
5	SC is simple, model-agnostic, and effective---but it is hitting a ceiling.
6	\citet{loo2025reevaluating} demonstrate that gains from increasing $K$ plateau and even decline for strong models.
7	The gap between SC's theoretical promise (independent correct voters converge to truth) and its practical behavior (saturating gains) points to a structural mismatch: same-model traces carry correlated errors, and majority voting has no mechanism to correct shared bias.
8	
9	Confidence-weighted \citep{taubenfeld2025cisc} and universal \citep{chen2024usc} extensions alleviate this, but the core limitation persists.
10	Recent neuro-symbolic methods \citep{pan2023logiclm, olausson2023linc, xu2026dylos, li2025aristotle, wang2025fover, luo2025logicagent} report gains on logical reasoning but introduce components beyond the reasoning model.
11	A simpler question precedes these: can the \emph{same} model extract useful formal constraints from its own traces---a zero-cost upgrade requiring no additional model or data?
12	No prior work has systematically evaluated this.
13	
14	We ground our analysis in the Condorcet Jury Theorem (CJT) \citep{condorcet1785}: if $K$ independent voters each answer correctly with probability $p > 0.5$, the majority converges to truth \citep{austensmith1996}.
15	When independence fails \citep{ladha1992correlated}, additional samples carry diminishing information.
16	Measuring inter-trace agreement with Fleiss' $\kappa$ \citep{fleiss1971kappa}, we find $\kappa = 0.21$--$0.82$ across instruction-tuned conditions, yielding Effective-$K = 1.20$--$3.62$ for nominal $K = 12$: traces beyond a dozen add negligible independent information (\S\ref{sec:effective-k}).
17	The bottleneck runs deeper than answer-level agreement suggests.
18	Chain-of-thought cosine similarity ($0.69$) far exceeds answer-level $\kappa$ ($0.41$), revealing that traces share reasoning processes even when they diverge on final answers (\S\ref{sec:process-kappa}).
19	When the same model extracts symbolic constraints from these correlated traces, the constraints exhibit \emph{symbolic confirmation bias}: on problems where the model errs, 83.3\% of self-extracted constraints support the wrong majority answer (bias ratio $4.97\times$), and 94\% of errors overlap with SC (\S\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}).
20	Self-extraction cannot supply the independent signal that CJT requires.
21	
22	We study this through Symbolic Invariant Consensus Aggregation (SICA): extract symbolic constraints from each of $K$ reasoning traces, deduplicate by syntactic equivalence, and select the answer best supported by a weighted partial MAX-SAT solution via Z3 \citep{demoura2008z3} (\S\ref{sec:pipeline}; Figure~\ref{fig:pipeline}).
23	We evaluate SICA across six models (7B--32B), three reasoning domains (FOLIO \citep{han2022folio}, ProofWriter \citep{tafjord2021proofwriter}, LogiQA \citep{liu2020logiqa}), and over 3,000 condition-evaluations (${\sim}$1,250 unique instances) with four-seed replication (Table~\ref{tab:main_results}).
24	Twenty-plus remediation strategies spanning fifteen families---from prompt engineering through method-level redesign---all produce neutral or negative results, confirming the bottleneck is persistent at the 7B--32B scale.
25	While prior work qualitatively concludes that LLMs cannot self-correct or self-verify without external feedback \citep{huang2024selfcorrect, stechly2025selfverification}, we contribute reusable quantitative diagnostics (Effective-$K$, bias ratio), show that formal Z3-checked verification also fails, and reveal process-level reasoning homogeneity invisible to answer-only metrics.
26	
27	Oracle controls confirm the aggregation mechanism is sound: gold logical annotations yield $+29.90$\,pp (Mistral-7B) and $+14.22$\,pp (Qwen-14B) on FOLIO ($p < 0.001$), yet self-extraction captures only ${\sim}$12\% of the same-model ceiling (\S\ref{sec:oracle}).
28	The central finding is a persistent independence-accuracy trade-off: Debate reduces $\kappa$ from 0.44 to 0.15 (tripling Eff-$K$) but collapses accuracy by $-18.6$\,pp; SC, Diverse-Prompt, and ICG all cluster at $\kappa \approx 0.40$--$0.47$, forming a same-model ceiling that holds across three model families (\S\ref{sec:remediation}).
29	A cross-architecture proof-of-concept suggests this gap is recoverable: DeBERTa-large NLI combined with SC yields $+3.50$\,pp on ProofWriter ($p = 0.013$; $+10.67$\,pp at $w{=}5$, $p < 0.001$), confirmed across three NLI architectures (\S\ref{sec:cross-arch}).
30	
31	Our contributions are:
32	\begin{enumerate}[nosep,leftmargin=*]
33	\item \textbf{Independence-Accuracy Trade-off.} Same-model $\kappa \geq 0.40$ across four methods and three model families; twenty-plus remediation strategies (fifteen families) fail. A cross-architecture NLI verifier ($+3.50$\,pp on ProofWriter, $p = 0.013$; confirmed across three NLI architectures) suggests the ceiling is escapable (\S\ref{sec:remediation}, \S\ref{sec:cross-arch}).
34	
35	\item \textbf{Symbolic Confirmation Bias.} On incorrect problems, 83.3\% of self-extracted constraints support the wrong majority answer (bias ratio $4.97\times$), and 94\% of errors overlap with SC (\S\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}).
36	
37	\item \textbf{Process-Level Independence.} Across four model families, CoT cosine similarity ($0.69$--$0.79$) far exceeds answer-level $\kappa$ ($0.45$--$0.74$); the gap intensifies with model capability, showing reasoning homogeneity is architecture-independent (\S\ref{sec:process-kappa}).
38	\end{enumerate}
39	
```

### Tool result

_source line 4768 · 2026-05-20T20:18:37.205Z_

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_01UDBnyyTHtXTNJe4kFgHGf3`</summary>

```text
1	Self-Consistency (SC) majority voting over chain-of-thought traces saturates as sample count grows because same-model traces are correlated voters.
2	We investigate whether symbolic constraint extraction can break this plateau through Symbolic Invariant Consensus Aggregation (SICA), which extracts symbolic constraints from reasoning traces and selects answers via weighted partial MAX-SAT.
3	Across four open-source LLMs (7B--32B) on formal and logical reasoning benchmarks---three reasoning domains with over 3,000 condition-evaluations (${\sim}$1,250 unique instances), we find that self-extracted constraints do not improve majority voting at any capacity level: none of sixteen non-degenerate conditions shows a significant positive effect; TOST equivalence tests (margin $\pm 2$\,pp) establish equivalence for four conditions, while five show genuine SICA--SC differences---four of which are negative.
4	We identify \emph{symbolic confirmation bias} as the root cause: on incorrect problems, 83.3\% of self-extracted constraints support the wrong majority answer (bias ratio $4.97\times$), and 94\% of errors overlap with SC.
5	Process-level analysis reveals a deeper bottleneck: chain-of-thought cosine similarity ($0.69$) far exceeds answer-level Fleiss' $\kappa$ ($0.41$), indicating that traces share reasoning processes even when they diverge on answers.
6	Twenty-plus same-model remediation strategies spanning fifteen families all fail, revealing a persistent independence-accuracy trade-off at the 7B--32B scale: methods that increase trace independence destroy accuracy (Debate: $\kappa = 0.15$, $-18.6$\,pp), while methods preserving accuracy cannot break the correlation floor ($\kappa \geq 0.40$ across three model families).
7	Oracle controls confirm the aggregation mechanism is sound ($+29.90$\,pp and $+14.22$\,pp on FOLIO, $+11.50$\,pp ProofWriter; $p < 0.001$); same-model self-extraction captures only ${\sim}$12\% of the oracle ceiling.
8	However, a cross-architecture proof-of-concept---DeBERTa-large NLI combined with SC---yields $+3.50$\,pp on ProofWriter ($p = 0.013$; $+10.67$\,pp at $w{=}5$, $p < 0.001$), confirmed across three NLI architectures, suggesting that the oracle gap is partially recoverable when verification signal comes from outside the reasoning model's distribution.
9	
```

</details>

TOST equivalence tests (margin $\\pm 2$\\,pp) establish equivalence for four conditions, while five show genuine SICA--SC differences---four of which are negative.\nWe identify \\emph{symbolic confirmation bias} as the root cause: on incorrect problems, 83.3\\% of self-extracted constraints support the wrong majority answer (bias ratio $4.97\\times$), and 94\\% of errors overlap with SC.\nProcess-level analysis reveals a deeper bottleneck: chain-of-thought cosine similarity ($0.69$) far exceeds answer-level Fleiss' $\\kappa$ ($0.41$), indicating that traces share reasoning processes even when they diverge on answers.\nTwenty-plus same-model remediation strategies spanning fifteen families all fail, revealing a persistent independence-accuracy trade-off at the 7B--32B scale: methods that increase trace independence destroy accuracy (Debate: $\\kappa = 0.15$, $-18.6$\\,pp), while methods preserving accuracy cannot break the correlation floor ($\\kappa \\geq 0.40$ across three model families).\nOracle controls confirm the aggregation mechanism is sound ($+29.90$\\,pp and $+14.22$\\,pp on FOLIO, $+11.50$\\,pp ProofWriter; $p < 0.001$); same-model self-extraction captures only ${\\sim}$12\\% of the oracle ceiling.\nHowever, a cross-architecture proof-of-concept---DeBERTa-large NLI combined with SC---yields $+3.50$\\,pp on ProofWriter ($p = 0.013$; $+10.67$\\,pp at $w{=}5$, $p < 0.001$), confirmed across three NLI architectures, suggesting that the oracle gap is partially recoverable when verification signal comes from outside the reasoning model's distribution.\n",

      {

          "+Across six open-source LLMs (7B--32B, from Mistral-7B at 54\\% SC to Qwen3-14B thinking mode at 86\\% SC) and three logical reasoning domains with over 3,000 condition-evaluations (${\\sim}$1,250 unique instances), we find that self-extracted constraints do not improve majority voting at any capacity level: none of sixteen non-degenerate conditions shows a significant positive effect; TOST equivalence tests (margin $\\pm 2$\\,pp) establish equivalence for four conditions, while five show genuine SICA--SC differences---four of which are negative.",
          " We identify \\emph{symbolic confirmation bias} as the root cause: on incorrect problems, 83.3\\% of self-extracted constraints support the wrong majority answer (bias ratio $4.97\\times$), and 94\\% of errors overlap with SC.",

    "newString": "When the same model extracts logical constraints from these correlated traces, the constraints exhibit \\emph{symbolic confirmation bias}",
    "originalFile": "\\section{Introduction}\\label{sec:introduction}\n\nTest-time computation scaling through multiple reasoning samples has emerged as a practical alternative to scaling model parameters \\citep{snell2025scaling, guo2025deepseekr1}.\nThe dominant aggregation strategy, Self-Consistency (SC) \\citep{wang2023sc}, samples $K$ chain-of-thought traces and selects the majority answer.\nSC is simple, model-agnostic, and effective---but it is hitting a ceiling.\n\\citet{loo2025reevaluating} demonstrate that gains from increasing $K$ plateau and even decline for strong models.\nThe gap between SC's theoretical promise (independent correct voters converge to truth) and its practical behavior (saturating gains) points to a structural mismatch: same-model traces carry correlated errors, and majority voting has no mechanism to correct shared bias.\n\nConfidence-weighted \\citep{taubenfeld2025cisc} and universal \\citep{chen2024usc} extensions alleviate this, but the core limitation persists.\nRecent neuro-symbolic methods \\citep{pan2023logiclm, olausson2023linc, xu2026dylos, li2025aristotle, wang2025fover, luo2025logicagent} report gains on logical reasoning but introduce components beyond the reasoning model.\nA simpler question precedes these: can the \\emph{same} model extract useful formal constraints from its own traces---a zero-cost upgrade requiring no additional model or data?\nNo prior work has systematically evaluated this.\n\nWe ground our analysis in the Condorcet Jury Theorem (CJT) \\citep{condorcet1785}: if $K$ independent voters each answer correctly with probability $p > 0.5$, the majority converges to truth \\citep{austensmith1996}.\nWhen independence fails \\citep{ladha1992correlated}, additional samples carry diminishing information.\nMeasuring inter-trace agreement with Fleiss' $\\kappa$ \\citep{fleiss1971kappa}, we find $\\kappa = 0.21$--$0.82$ across instruction-tuned conditions, yielding Effective-$K = 1.20$--$3.62$ for nominal $K = 12$: traces beyond a dozen add negligible independent information (\\S\\ref{sec:effective-k}).\nThe bottleneck runs deeper than answer-level agreement suggests.\nChain-of-thought cosine similarity ($0.69$) far exceeds answer-level $\\kappa$ ($0.41$), revealing that traces share reasoning processes even when they diverge on final answers (\\S\\ref{sec:process-kappa}).\nWhen the same model extracts symbolic constraints from these correlated traces, the constraints exhibit \\emph{symbolic confirmation bias}: on problems where the model errs, 83.3\\% of self-extracted constraints support the wrong majority answer (bias ratio $4.97\\times$), and 94\\% of errors overlap with SC (\\S\\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}).\nSelf-extraction cannot supply the independent signal that CJT requires.\n\nWe study this through Symbolic Invariant Consensus Aggregation (SICA): extract symbolic constraints from each of $K$ reasoning traces, deduplicate by syntactic equivalence, and select the answer best supported by a weighted partial MAX-SAT solution via Z3 \\citep{demoura2008z3} (\\S\\ref{sec:pipeline}; Figure~\\ref{fig:pipeline}).\nWe evaluate SICA across six models (7B--32B), three reasoning domains (FOLIO \\citep{han2022folio}, ProofWriter \\citep{tafjord2021proofwriter}, LogiQA \\citep{liu2020logiqa}), and over 3,000 condition-evaluations (${\\sim}$1,250 unique instances) with four-seed replication (Table~\\ref{tab:main_results}).\nTwenty-plus remediation strategies spanning fifteen families---from prompt engineering through method-level redesign---all produce neutral or negative results, confirming the bottleneck is persistent at the 7B--32B scale.\nWhile prior work qualitatively concludes that LLMs cannot self-correct or self-verify without external feedback \\citep{huang2024selfcorrect, stechly2025selfverification}, we contribute reusable quantitative diagnostics (Effective-$K$, bias ratio), show that formal Z3-checked verification also fails, and reveal process-level reasoning homogeneity invisible to answer-only metrics.\n\nOracle controls confirm the aggregation mechanism is sound: gold logical annotations yield $+29.90$\\,pp (Mistral-7B) and $+14.22$\\,pp (Qwen-14B) on FOLIO ($p < 0.001$), yet self-extraction captures only ${\\sim}$12\\% of the same-model ceiling (\\S\\ref{sec:oracle}).\nThe central finding is a persistent independence-accuracy trade-off: Debate reduces $\\kappa$ from 0.44 to 0.15 (tripling Eff-$K$) but collapses accuracy by $-18.6$\\,pp; SC, Diverse-Prompt, and ICG all cluster at $\\kappa \\approx 0.40$--$0.47$, forming a same-model ceiling that holds across three model families (\\S\\ref{sec:remediation}).\nA cross-architecture proof-of-concept suggests this gap is recoverable: DeBERTa-large NLI combined with SC yields $+3.50$\\,pp on ProofWriter ($p = 0.013$; $+10.67$\\,pp at $w{=}5$, $p < 0.001$), confirmed across three NLI architectures (\\S\\ref{sec:cross-arch}).\n\nOur contributions are:\n\\begin{enumerate}[nosep,leftmargin=*]\n\\item \\textbf{Independence-Accuracy Trade-off.} Same-model $\\kappa \\geq 0.40$ across four methods and three model families; twenty-plus remediation strategies (fifteen families) fail. A cross-architecture NLI verifier ($+3.50$\\,pp on ProofWriter, $p = 0.013$; confirmed across three NLI architectures) suggests the ceiling is escapable (\\S\\ref{sec:remediation}, \\S\\ref{sec:cross-arch}).\n\n\\item \\textbf{Symbolic Confirmation Bias.} On incorrect problems, 83.3\\% of self-extracted constraints support the wrong majority answer (bias ratio $4.97\\times$), and 94\\% of errors overlap with SC (\\S\\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}).\n\n\\item \\textbf{Process-Level Independence.} Across four model families, CoT cosine similarity ($0.69$--$0.79$) far exceeds answer-level $\\kappa$ ($0.45$--$0.74$); the gap intensifies with model capability, showing reasoning homogeneity is architecture-independent (\\S\\ref{sec:process-kappa}).\n\\end{enumerate}\n",

      {

    "originalFile": "\\section{Introduction}\\label{sec:introduction}\n\nTest-time computation scaling through multiple reasoning samples has emerged as a practical alternative to scaling model parameters \\citep{snell2025scaling, guo2025deepseekr1}.\nThe dominant aggregation strategy, Self-Consistency (SC) \\citep{wang2023sc}, samples $K$ chain-of-thought traces and selects the majority answer.\nSC is simple, model-agnostic, and effective---but it is hitting a ceiling.\n\\citet{loo2025reevaluating} demonstrate that gains from increasing $K$ plateau and even decline for strong models.\nThe gap between SC's theoretical promise (independent correct voters converge to truth) and its practical behavior (saturating gains) points to a structural mismatch: same-model traces carry correlated errors, and majority voting has no mechanism to correct shared bias.\n\nConfidence-weighted \\citep{taubenfeld2025cisc} and universal \\citep{chen2024usc} extensions alleviate this, but the core limitation persists.\nRecent neuro-symbolic methods \\citep{pan2023logiclm, olausson2023linc, xu2026dylos, li2025aristotle, wang2025fover, luo2025logicagent} report gains on logical reasoning but introduce components beyond the reasoning model.\nA simpler question precedes these: can the \\emph{same} model extract useful formal constraints from its own traces---a zero-cost upgrade requiring no additional model or data?\nNo prior work has systematically evaluated this.\n\nWe ground our analysis in the Condorcet Jury Theorem (CJT) \\citep{condorcet1785}: if $K$ independent voters each answer correctly with probability $p > 0.5$, the majority converges to truth \\citep{austensmith1996}.\nWhen independence fails \\citep{ladha1992correlated}, additional samples carry diminishing information.\nMeasuring inter-trace agreement with Fleiss' $\\kappa$ \\citep{fleiss1971kappa}, we find $\\kappa = 0.21$--$0.82$ across instruction-tuned conditions, yielding Effective-$K = 1.20$--$3.62$ for nominal $K = 12$: traces beyond a dozen add negligible independent information (\\S\\ref{sec:effective-k}).\nThe bottleneck runs deeper than answer-level agreement suggests.\nChain-of-thought cosine similarity ($0.69$) far exceeds answer-level $\\kappa$ ($0.41$), revealing that traces share reasoning processes even when they diverge on final answers (\\S\\ref{sec:process-kappa}).\nWhen the same model extracts logical constraints from these correlated traces, the constraints exhibit \\emph{symbolic confirmation bias}: on problems where the model errs, 83.3\\% of self-extracted constraints support the wrong majority answer (bias ratio $4.97\\times$), and 94\\% of errors overlap with SC (\\S\\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}).\nSelf-extraction cannot supply the independent signal that CJT requires.\n\nWe study this through Symbolic Invariant Consensus Aggregation (SICA): extract symbolic constraints from each of $K$ reasoning traces, deduplicate by syntactic equivalence, and select the answer best supported by a weighted partial MAX-SAT solution via Z3 \\citep{demoura2008z3} (\\S\\ref{sec:pipeline}; Figure~\\ref{fig:pipeline}).\nWe evaluate SICA across six models (7B--32B), three reasoning domains (FOLIO \\citep{han2022folio}, ProofWriter \\citep{tafjord2021proofwriter}, LogiQA \\citep{liu2020logiqa}), and over 3,000 condition-evaluations (${\\sim}$1,250 unique instances) with four-seed replication (Table~\\ref{tab:main_results}).\nTwenty-plus remediation strategies spanning fifteen families---from prompt engineering through method-level redesign---all produce neutral or negative results, confirming the bottleneck is persistent at the 7B--32B scale.\nWhile prior work qualitatively concludes that LLMs cannot self-correct or self-verify without external feedback \\citep{huang2024selfcorrect, stechly2025selfverification}, we contribute reusable quantitative diagnostics (Effective-$K$, bias ratio), show that formal Z3-checked verification also fails, and reveal process-level reasoning homogeneity invisible to answer-only metrics.\n\nOracle controls confirm the aggregation mechanism is sound: gold logical annotations yield $+29.90$\\,pp (Mistral-7B) and $+14.22$\\,pp (Qwen-14B) on FOLIO ($p < 0.001$), yet self-extraction captures only ${\\sim}$12\\% of the same-model ceiling (\\S\\ref{sec:oracle}).\nThe central finding is a persistent independence-accuracy trade-off: Debate reduces $\\kappa$ from 0.44 to 0.15 (tripling Eff-$K$) but collapses accuracy by $-18.6$\\,pp; SC, Diverse-Prompt, and ICG all cluster at $\\kappa \\approx 0.40$--$0.47$, forming a same-model ceiling that holds across three model families (\\S\\ref{sec:remediation}).\nA cross-architecture proof-of-concept suggests this gap is recoverable: DeBERTa-large NLI combined with SC yields $+3.50$\\,pp on ProofWriter ($p = 0.013$; $+10.67$\\,pp at $w{=}5$, $p < 0.001$), confirmed across three NLI architectures (\\S\\ref{sec:cross-arch}).\n\nOur contributions are:\n\\begin{enumerate}[nosep,leftmargin=*]\n\\item \\textbf{Independence-Accuracy Trade-off.} Same-model $\\kappa \\geq 0.40$ across four methods and three model families; twenty-plus remediation strategies (fifteen families) fail. A cross-architecture NLI verifier ($+3.50$\\,pp on ProofWriter, $p = 0.013$; confirmed across three NLI architectures) suggests the ceiling is escapable (\\S\\ref{sec:remediation}, \\S\\ref{sec:cross-arch}).\n\n\\item \\textbf{Symbolic Confirmation Bias.} On incorrect problems, 83.3\\% of self-extracted constraints support the wrong majority answer (bias ratio $4.97\\times$), and 94\\% of errors overlap with SC (\\S\\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}).\n\n\\item \\textbf{Process-Level Independence.} Across four model families, CoT cosine similarity ($0.69$--$0.79$) far exceeds answer-level $\\kappa$ ($0.45$--$0.74$); the gap intensifies with model capability, showing reasoning homogeneity is architecture-independent (\\S\\ref{sec:process-kappa}).\n\\end{enumerate}\n",

Across all conditions at $w{=}3$, combo gain tracks the capability gap (Figure~\\ref{fig:capability-gap}): meaningful improvement emerges only when the verifier's standalone accuracy approaches or exceeds the generator's SC baseline.\"\n\n**Patch 5: Table 1 — Seed 标注 (R8-W2)**\n\n文件: `experiments.tex`, Table 1\n\n不加新列（节省空间）。在有 multi-seed 的条件 Model 列加 superscript:\n- Mistral-7B FOLIO (default) 行加 $^{4s}$（4 seeds）\n- Mistral-7B FOLIO (s123), (s456), (s789) 三行不变（它们本身就是各 seed 行）\n\n在 Table caption 末尾添加: \"$^{4s}$\\,4-seed replication (confirmatory); unmarked single-seed conditions are exploratory.\"\n\n**Patch 6: Figure 交换 (R8-I2)**\n\n- 将主文 Figure 2 (process_kappa scatter, `fig3_process_kappa_scatter.pdf`) 移到 Appendix\n- 将 Appendix 中 Figure 5 (intervention strategies summary) 移到主文 §5.3 Remediation Analysis 之后\n- 调整所有 Figure 编号和 `\\ref` 引用使其一致\n- 进主文的 Figure 使用 `width=0.85\\columnwidth`\n\n注意: 如果 Figure 5 在 Appendix 中的实际文件名/label 不同，先找到它确认。\n\n**Patch 7: 全文术语统一 (R8-N1)**\n\n全文搜索 \"FOL constraints\" 和 \"FOL constraint\"，替换为 \"logical constraints\"/\"logical constraint\"。\n\n**保留 \"first-order logic\" 的位置**:\n- method.tex §3.2 描述 extraction prompt 格式时: \"We prompt the model to extract premises as first-order logic formulas\"（或类似描述）\n- 第一次出现时解释: \"prompted as first-order logic (FOL) formulas; in practice, most extracted constraints are propositional ground clauses (quantifier statistics in Appendix~\\ref{app:quantifier})\"\n\n**不改的**:\n- \"symbolic confirmation bias\" — 这是我们的术语，保留\n- \"Symbolic Mirage\" — 假设名称，保留\n- \"Symbolic Invariant Consensus Aggregation\" — 方法名，保留\n- method.tex Figure 1 caption 中可以保留 \"FOL\" 如果它指的是 prompt 格式\n\n---\n\n**执行顺序**: Patch 1-5 可并行。Patch 6 需要先定位 Figure 5 在 Appendix 中的确切位置。Patch 7 在其他 Patch 完成后做全文扫描。\n\n**完成后**: 列出所有修改的文件和行号，以及 page count 是否仍在 8pp 内。不要做任何未列出的修改。",

## R8 数据提取结果（2/3 完成）

### W6 Seed Annotations ✅
31 个条件已记录。所有主实验使用 seed=42，Z3 solver seed 全局 hardcoded=42。
4 个多 seed 组：
- Mistral FOLIO: s42/789
- Qwen14B FOLIO: s42/123
- Qwen7B LogiQA: s42/123/456
- ICG Mistral: s42/123/456

### W7 Dedup Ablation ✅ (Mistral-7B FOLIO-204)
| Dedup Method | Avg Constraints | Accuracy |
|-------------|----------------|----------|
| No dedup | 69.0 | 54.41% |
| String dedup | 45.4 | 55.88% |
| Z3 dedup | 39.3 | **59.31%** |

- Z3 dedup 比 string match 多抓 ~10% semantic duplicates
- No-dedup vs Z3-dedup: **p=0.013** (McNemar, 显著)
- String vs Z3: p=0.065（边缘显著）

### R8-W3 Dedup Statistics ✅ (11 个条件, 3 benchmarks)
| Benchmark | Avg Pre | Avg Post | Dedup Ratio |
|-----------|---------|----------|-------------|
| FOLIO (6 models) | 65.0 | 33.2 | **46.4%** |
| ProofWriter (3 models) | 134.1 | 43.8 | **63.3%** |
| LogiQA (2 models) | 48.1 | 39.2 | **18.8%** |

全局平均 dedup ratio = 46.0%（范围 15.3%–75.8%）。ProofWriter 冗余最高（规则化前提），LogiQA 最低（自然语言前提）。
